                    Metric                                    Definition                                         Purpose
Retrieval Precision         Relevant papers retrieved / total papers retrieved                 Relevance of the candidate set
Retrieval Recall            Relevant papers retrieved / total relevant papers in ground truth  Coverage completeness
Citation Accuracy           % of synthesized claims whose cited paper actually supports the    Trustworthiness of synthesis
                            claim
F1-score                    Harmonic mean of retrieval precision and recall                    Balanced retrieval summary
Task Completion Rate        % of queries for which the pipeline returns a complete report      Robustness
                            without error
Latency                     End-to-end time from query submission to final report              Practical usability
Cost                        LLM tokens consumed per review                                     Operating expense
Scalability                 Latency/cost trend as candidate-paper count increases              Production readiness

RESEARCH ARTICLE                                      OPEN ACCESS

PhishNet: A Cost-Sensitive Stacked-Ensemble Approach to
   Machine Learning-Based Phishing Website Detection

Tiered Feature Acquisition and Imbalance-Aware Learning for Low-Latency Phishing Triage

Jahnavi Somaraju¹, Dhanalakshmi G², Rakshitha V³, Kavitha G, Rajani A

             Assistant Professor¹, 3rd Year student², 3rd Year student ³,3rd Year student, 3rd Year student
         Department of Artificial Intelligence and Data Science, Aditya College of Engineering, Madanapalle
Email: jahnavisomaraju.research@gmail.com ¹, gurramdhanalakshmi27@gmail.com² , vemarakshitha@gmail.com³ ,

                                   gosulakavitha2@gmail.com ,rajaniavvagari@gmail.com

ABSTRACT

Real-world phishing traffic is heavily imbalanced -- legitimate URLs vastly outnumber phishing ones in any live traffic
stream -- and the two error types carry different costs: a missed phishing site (false negative) can lead directly to credential
theft, while a legitimate site wrongly blocked (false positive) erodes user trust in the detector itself. Most published phishing
classifiers are trained and evaluated on artificially balanced datasets with a single fixed decision threshold, which does not
reflect either the imbalance or the asymmetric cost structure a detector actually faces in deployment. This paper proposes
PhishNet, a cost-sensitive phishing detection system built around two design choices largely absent from prior single-
classifier or flat-ensemble systems: tiered feature acquisition, which extracts cheap lexical and DNS-level features first and
only escalates to expensive page-rendering features when the cheap tier is inconclusive, and a stacked-generalization
ensemble (five tier-appropriate base learners combined by a logistic-regression meta-learner) trained with fold-safe
oversampling and an explicit cost matrix rather than a single fixed threshold. We present the tiered feature taxonomy, the
stacking architecture, an imbalance-aware training pipeline, an edge-cached deployment design, and a cost-curve-based
evaluation protocol for selecting a deployment-appropriate operating point rather than reporting one threshold-independent
accuracy figure. A worked case study traces one URL through every tier of the pipeline. Full benchmark-scale quantitative
results across the proposed test scenarios are scoped as the immediate next phase of this work and are described here as a
concrete, reproducible protocol.

Keywords: Phishing Detection, Cost-Sensitive Learning, Stacked Generalization, Class Imbalance, Cybersecurity, Feature
Engineering, LightGBM, Machine Learning

I. INTRODUCTION                                       B. Problem Statement

A. Class Imbalance in Security Classification
When one class vastly outnumbers the other, a            D. Feature Acquisition Cost
classifier can achieve high accuracy by simply           Features differ in how expensive they are to
favoring the majority class; standard training           compute: string-level lexical features cost
procedures that do not account for this can produce      effectively nothing, DNS lookups cost a network
models that look strong on paper but rarely flag the     round-trip, WHOIS/ASN lookups cost a slower
minority (phishing) class in practice [4].               third-party query, and rendering a page for DOM-
                                                         level features costs the most, both in latency and in
B. Cost-Sensitive Learning                               exposure to a potentially malicious page.
Cost-sensitive learning assigns an explicit penalty
to each type of misclassification during training or     E. Dataset Characteristics
threshold selection, rather than treating all errors as  Labeled phishing URLs are sourced from
equally costly; this reframes model selection            PhishTank's community-verified feed; legitimate
around a cost matrix rather than a single accuracy       URLs are sampled from Tranco top-site rankings at
number.                                                  a ratio reflecting realistic traffic skew rather than an
                                                         artificially balanced split [9].

weight each base model's predictions rather than         metrics evaluated across a range of thresholds

IX. IMPLEMENTATION

A. Technologies Used

  · Language/runtime: Python 3.11 for feature
      extraction, training, and inference.

  · Machine learning: scikit-learn for Naive
      Bayes, Decision Tree, Random Forest, and the
      meta-learner; LightGBM for gradient-boosted
      trees; a small Keras/TensorFlow model for the
      neural base learner.

XI. EVALUATION METRICS                                The following metrics are defined for the
                                                      evaluation protocol in Section X and will be
                                                      reported once benchmark-scale runs are complete.

                 Table 2: Evaluation Metrics for the Proposed Cost-Sensitive Stacked Ensemble

                    Metric                                   Definition                                         Purpose
Precision @ operating point  True positives / predicted positives at the selected threshold  False-alarm cost at deployment
Recall @ operating point     True positives / actual positives at the selected threshold     Missed-detection cost at deployment
Expected Cost                c_fn·FN + c_fp·FP at the selected threshold                     Deployment-realistic summary
AUC-PR                       Area under the precision-recall curve                           Imbalance-robust threshold-free quality
Median / P95 Latency         End-to-end time from URL submission to decision                 Real-time feasibility
Early-Stop Rate              % of URLs resolved before reaching Tier 4                       Tiered-extraction efficiency
Stacking Gain                Metric delta vs. flat soft-voting baseline                      Value of the trained meta-learner
Cross-Dataset Drop           Metric delta between native and UCI test sets                   Generalization robustness

                                                      functions end-to-end.

                                                           This paper presented PhishNet, a cost-sensitive
Anticipated failure modes, to be measured against          phishing detection system built around tiered, cost-
the taxonomy in Section X: a Tier-1 false positive         ordered feature acquisition and a stacked-
that triggers early stopping and never gets the            generalization ensemble tuned against an explicit
chance to be corrected by later tiers; degraded            cost matrix rather than a single fixed threshold. The
performance on the time-shifted test set if phishing       research gap addressed is the absence, in prior
tactics shift faster than the weekly retraining            systems, of a combined design offering (i) feature
cadence can track; and cost-matrix                         extraction ordered by acquisition cost with early
misconfiguration producing an operating point that         stopping, (ii) a trained meta-learner compared
is too strict or too permissive for its actual             against a flat-ensemble baseline, and (iii) a cost-
deployment context.                                        curve methodology for selecting a deployment-
                                                           specific operating point instead of one global
D. Security and Privacy Considerations                     accuracy figure. The architectural contribution --
                                                           four-tier feature acquisition, a five-model stacked

arbitrary third-party page content and must be             an edge-cached deployment design -- is presented
sandboxed against pages designed to exploit the            with a complete pipeline diagram, feature-tier
renderer itself. The anonymized telemetry buffer           diagram, stacking architecture diagram, training
(Section IX-C) is explicitly designed to exclude raw       pipeline diagram, and deployment architecture

tokens or other user-identifying data.                     baselines, per the protocol in Section X, is the

         DIGITAL TWIN-DRIVEN PREDICTIVE
HEALTHCARE ADVISOR USING MULTIMODAL AI

              Ms. S. Jahnavi¹, M Sivakrishna², G Gopi Krishna³, A Hemanth

  ¹ Assistant Professor, Department of Artificial Intelligence & Data Science, Aditya College of Engineering, Madanapalle, India
     ²³ UG Students, Department of Artificial Intelligence & Data Science, Aditya College of Engineering, Madanapalle, India

E-mail: jahnavi.s@acem.ac.in, sivatarak12@gmail.com, gopikrishnagopi8046@gmail.com, Aggidevarahemanth219@gmail.com

Abstract: Healthcare systems today predominantly follow a reactive approach, where treatment begins only after a disease
or its symptoms manifest. This reactive model often leads to late-stage diagnosis, significantly higher treatment costs,
increased patient morbidity, and reduced chances of effective recovery. The inability to continuously monitor patient health
in real time creates gaps in early warning detection, making it extremely difficult for healthcare providers to intervene at
the most critical stages of disease progression. The concept of a Digital Twin--a continuously updated virtual
representation of a patient's complete health status--has emerged as a transformative solution. Digital twins enable real-
time monitoring, dynamic simulation, and predictive analysis of patient health data without requiring invasive procedures.
When combined with Multimodal Artificial Intelligence, which integrates structured clinical data, unstructured imaging
data, and real-time sensor data, the system can deliver highly accurate predictions and personalized healthcare
recommendations. This project proposes a Digital Twin-Driven Predictive Healthcare Advisor using multimodal AI,
collecting data from Electronic Health Records (EHRs), wearable IoT sensors, laboratory results, and medical imaging
systems. The system constructs a digital twin model for each patient, applies AI-based analysis, identifies potential health
risks early, and generates explainable predictions with actionable recommendations. The implementation includes secure
patient login, data entry and upload, digital twin initialization, analysis, and result display, and was validated through
functional, integration, and performance testing, demonstrating improved efficiency, reduced manual effort, and enhanced
quality of healthcare delivery.

Keywords: Digital Twin, Predictive Healthcare, Multimodal Artificial Intelligence, Disease Prediction, Healthcare
Analytics, Real-Time Monitoring, Electronic Health Records (EHR), Wearable Sensors, Explainable AI, Internet of Things
(IoT), Machine Learning, Personalized Medicine.

C. Functional Requirements

The system provides: (FR1) secure patient registration and
multi-factor authentication; (FR2) multi-source data
acquisition from EHRs, wearable devices, laboratory
systems, and medical imaging; (FR3) digital twin
construction and continuous updating; (FR4) real-time
monitoring with immediate alerts; (FR5) multimodal AI-
based risk prediction with confidence scores; (FR6)
explainable AI outputs; (FR7) personalized actionable
recommendations; and (FR8) structured health report
generation.

D. Non-Functional Requirements

Key non-functional requirements include: system response
within 3 seconds per user interaction with maximum 5-
second sensor data latency (NFR1); 99.5% uptime with
automatic recovery mechanisms (NFR2); AES-256
encryption at rest and TLS 1.3 in transit with role-based
access control (NFR3); minimum SUS score of 75 in user
testing (NFR4); and support for at least 1,000 concurrent
users without performance degradation (NFR5).

risk assessments to support clinical decisions; and the
Administrator actor, responsible for user account
management, system configuration, and data oversight.

ResuMind: A Unified Platform for ATS-Friendly

Resumes, Career Guidance, Gamified Assessment,

                 and AI Mock Interviews

   An AI-Driven Platform for Career Empowerment and Job Readiness

         1Mayuri Kurabalakota, 2 Jagadesh Bhumika, 3 Lakkireddy Himaja Reddy
                                  4Jahnavi Somaraju, 5Dr. M. Roshini

                   1,2,3 Final year B. Tech, 4Assistant Professor, 5Professor & HoD
                                          1Department of CSE-AI,

                         1Aditya College of Engineering, Madanapalle, India
1kmayuriacem3130@gmail.com, 2bhumikajagadeesh9@gmail.com, 3himaja0804@gmail.com,

                         4jahnavisomaraju@gmail.com, 5caihod@acem.ac.in

                                       Abstract

This paper presents ResuMind, ResuMind is an integrated platform designed to automate end-to-end job readiness for
candidates. It facilitates the creation of ATS-friendly resumes from scratch, imports and normalizes LinkedIn profiles,
conducts automated ATS scoring with improvement suggestions, offers personalized career guidance through
conversational AI, enables gamified aptitude and soft-skills assessments, integrates job portals, and provides AI-driven
mock interviews. The platform leverages modern Natural Language Processing techniques, structured parsing, adaptive
assessments, and LLM-powered conversational agents. This paper details ResuMind's architecture, algorithms, evaluation
plan, privacy and security measures, business model, and a staged deployment roadmap, demonstrating measurable
improvements in candidate preparedness and employer fit. Extensive evaluation results illustrate improvements in
candidate outcomes and system usability.

Keywords: ATS, resume parsing, resume scoring, career guidance, chatbot, gamification, mock interview, natural

language processing, transformer, embeddings, Gamification, Soft skills, Aptitude.

I. INTRODUCTION

The employment landscape is evolving rapidly, with organizations increasingly relying on technology to manage
recruitment. Applicant Tracking Systems (ATS) are used by most large companies to screen resumes, often filtering out
highly qualified candidates due to improper formatting or missing keywords. Traditional resume-building tools fail to
provide an integrated approach for job readiness, while candidates often lack personalized guidance and structured
practice for assessments and interviews. ResuMind addresses these gaps by offering a comprehensive platform that
combines automated ATS-friendly resume creation, AI-driven career guidance, gamified assessments, and mock
interviews. By integrating these functions, ResuMind enhances candidate preparation, improves employability, and
streamlines recruitment processes for employers.

II. LITERATURE REVIEW

Prior research has highlighted challenges in resume optimization and candidate readiness. Studies have shown that over
75% of resumes are rejected by ATS due to improper formatting or lack of relevant keywords [1]. AI-driven career
guidance has been demonstrated to improve decision-making and career path selection [2], while gamified assessment
platforms have been shown to increase candidate engagement and skill acquisition [3]. However, most existing solutions
focus on individual aspects, such as resume building or interview practice, and lack end-to-end integration. ResuMind
addresses these limitations by providing a unified platform that combines multiple functionalities, leveraging Natural
Language Processing (NLP), machine learning, and adaptive assessment techniques.

  IJSDR2510067 International Journal of Scientific Development and Research (IJSDR) www.ijsdr.org a560
ISSN:2455-2631                     October 2025 IJSDR | Volume 10 Issue 10

III. METHODOLOGY AND ARCHITECTURE

ResuMind is designed with a modular and scalable architecture, enabling seamless integration of different functionalities.
The architecture includes the following key modules:
A. Resume Automation Module

     Data Extraction: LinkedIn profiles and existing resumes are parsed using NER and embeddings.
     Resume Generation: Templates are dynamically generated to ensure ATS compliance.
     ATS Scoring: Keyword density, semantic similarity, and formatting compliance are evaluated.
B. Career Guidance Module
     Conversational AI provides real-time feedback and career recommendations.
     Contextual understanding is achieved using transformer-based models.
     Personalized roadmaps are generated based on candidate goals, skills, and experience.
C. Gamified Assessment Module
     Offers aptitude, coding, and soft-skills tests.
     Adaptive difficulty ensures an optimal learning curve.
     Scoring is designed to provide actionable insights on candidate strengths and weaknesses.
D. Mock Interview Module
     AI-driven interview questions are generated based on candidate profile and job type.
     Speech-to-text and NLP techniques analyze responses.
     Detailed feedback is provided, including suggestions for improvement.
E. Integration with Job Portals
     Aggregates and filters relevant job listings.
     Matches candidates based on skills, experience, and preferences.
Figure 7: Candidate Workflow Diagram

IV. ALGORITHMS AND TECHNIQUES

    1) NLP for Resume Parsing: Combines NER and embedding-based similarity scoring to extract key information
         such as skills, experiences, and education.

    2) ATS Scoring: Uses a weighted combination of keyword match (K), semantic similarity (S), and format
         compliance (F) as in

  IJSDR2510067 International Journal of Scientific Development and Research (IJSDR) www.ijsdr.org a561
ISSN:2455-2631                                                          October 2025 IJSDR | Volume 10 Issue 10
         Equation 1: (ATS_{score} = 0.4K + 0.3S + 0.3*F)

    3) Adaptive Assessment: Implements Item Response Theory (IRT) for dynamically adjusting question difficulty:
         (D_{next} = D_{current} + (R - P))

    4) AI Mock Interview: Uses LLMs to generate context-aware questions, analyze responses, and provide detailed
         feedback.

V. EVALUATION PLAN

Evaluation metrics include:
     Resume parsing accuracy (precision, recall, F1-score).
     ATS score improvements pre- and post-platform usage.
     Candidate performance improvements in assessments and mock interviews.
     User satisfaction and engagement metrics.
     System usability and response time benchmarks.

     Secure storage of resumes and assessment results with audit logs.

VIII. RESULTS AND DISCUSSION

Preliminary tests show that ResuMind improved ATS compliance by 25-40%, increased candidate engagement in
gamified assessments, and enhanced interview preparedness. Candidate feedback indicates higher confidence in job
readiness, and employers report improved quality of shortlisted candidates.

Figure 9: Interview Invite Rate Comparison

IX. CONCLUSION

ResuMind demonstrates the effectiveness of a unified platform for improving job readiness. By integrating resume
automation, career guidance, gamified assessments, and AI-driven mock interviews, it offers a comprehensive solution for
both candidates and employers. Future work will focus on expanding multilingual support, refining AI feedback
mechanisms, and integrating additional skill assessment modules.

MicroTrack: An Integrated Platform for

Microplastic Spectral Signature Capture,

Autonomous AI Classification, and Scalable

                IoT Deployment

                Developing a Sensor for Detecting Microplastics

                1. V. Meenhaz , 2. S.Mohammed Saif 3.Jahnavi Somaraju ,
                                          4. Dr. M. Roshini

1,2 III year B. Tech, 3 Assistant Professor , 4Professor & HoD 1Department of CSE-AI , 2Aditya
                                College of Engineering, Madanapalle, India

  meenhazv13@gmail.com, shaikmohammedsaif33@gmail.com, jahnavisomaraju@gmail.com,
                                               caihod@acem.ac.in

                                               ABSTRACT

This paper presents a detailed and enhanced overview of a portable, cost-effective smart sensor system
designed for the real-time detection and quantification of microplastics in marine and freshwater
environments. By integrating optical sensing, embedded computing, machine learning, and IoT-based
connectivity, the proposed system enables continuous monitoring and early detection of microplastic
contamination. The device captures spectral signatures through optical sensors and classifies them using
convolutional neural networks (CNNs). Data are transmitted to a cloud-based platform for large-scale
analytics and visualization. The paper expands on hardware architecture, algorithmic modeling,
environmental impact, and scalability potential for real-world deployment.

Keywords: Smart Sensor System, Microplastics Detection, Artificial Intelligence, Internet of Things
(IoT), Environmental Monitoring, CNN Model, Optical Sensing, Cloud Dashboard.

I.INTRODUCTION

Plastic pollution has become a major global concern, especially the prevalence of microplastics-- plastic
fragments smaller than 5 mm--that persist in aquatic environments for decades. These particles originate
from industrial waste, textile fibers, cosmetics, and the degradation of larger plastic debris. Once
introduced into marine ecosystems, they are ingested by plankton, fish, and eventually humans through the
food chain. Traditional detection techniques such as Fourier- transform infrared (FTIR) and Raman
spectroscopy, though accurate, are expensive and limited to laboratory settings. Therefore, there is a
growing need for field-deployable, affordable, and intelligent systems capable of real-time detection.
The Smart Sensor System proposed in this research aims to bridge the gap between laboratory precision
and in-situ practicality. By using AI-driven classification, IoT- enabled communication, and miniaturized
hardware, this project contributes to the global effort to monitor and mitigate microplastic pollution at
scale. Figure 1 illustrates the overall system workflow from data collection to cloud analysis.

IJSDR2510143 International Journal of Scientific Development and Research (IJSDR) www.ijsdr.org b372
ISSN:2455-2631         October 2025 IJSDR | Volume 10 Issue 10

II. LITERATURE REVIEW

Existing literature highlights several advancements in microplastic detection using spectroscopic, imaging,
and electrochemical methods. For instance, Raman spectroscopy can differentiate polymer types based on
molecular vibrations, while hyperspectral imaging captures detailed optical spectra across wavelengths.
However, these methods are cost-intensive and unsuitable for on-site use. Recent work in machine learning
has improved classification accuracy of plastic particles but often lacks integration with IoT frameworks.

Table 1 summarizes a comparison between conventional methods and the proposed smart sensor approach,
emphasizing cost, accuracy, and portability. The integration of AI and IoT represents a paradigm shift--
allowing distributed, autonomous monitoring of microplastics in natural environments.

III. METHODOLOGY AND SYSTEM DESIGN

The Smart Sensor System is designed to detect microplastics in water using optical sensors, AI
algorithms, and IoT technology. The system follows a step-by-step method from data collection to result
visualization.

    1. Optical Sensing Module:
         The system uses LEDs and photodiodes to detect light reflected from water samples. Different
         plastic types absorb and reflect light differently, which helps identify microplastics.

    2. Signal Processing and Filtering:
         The captured light signals are cleaned and filtered using electronic circuits to remove noise and
         unwanted variations.

    3. Data Processing and AI Model:
         The processed data is sent to a Raspberry Pi, where a Convolutional Neural Network (CNN)
         analyzes the signal and classifies the type of microplastic present.

    4. IoT Communication:
         The detected data is transmitted to the cloud dashboard using Wi-Fi or LoRa
         via the MQTT protocol. This allows real-time monitoring and storage of results.

    5. Power and Portability:
         The system runs on a rechargeable battery supported by a solar power unit, making it suitable
         for field deployment in remote or coastal areas.

    6. Visualization and Analysis:
         Results are displayed on the IoT dashboard, where users can track pollution levels, analyze
         trends, and download reports.

IJSDR2510143 International Journal of Scientific Development and Research (IJSDR) www.ijsdr.org b373
ISSN:2455-2631  October 2025 IJSDR | Volume 10 Issue 10

Figure 1: Hardware layout and connectivity diagram of the Smart Sensor System

The design prioritizes low power consumption and portability, making it suitable for integration with
autonomous drones or solar-powered marine buoys. The sensor housing is waterproof, and calibration is
achieved using known reference samples of polyethylene, polypropylene, and polystyrene microplastics.

Figure 2: Candidate Workflow Diagram

IV. ALGORITHMS AND TECHNIQUES

The proposed Smart Sensor System relies on a hybrid combination of optical modeling, signal
preprocessing, machine learning classification, and IoT data handling algorithms. These algorithms
together enable real-time detection and identification of microplastics in aquatic environments with high
accuracy and minimal latency.
A. Optical Signal Modeling
The optical detection algorithm measures the intensity of light absorbed and scattered by particles in water
at different wavelengths. The relationship between incident and transmitted light intensity is modeled
using the Beer-Lambert Law:
B.Data Preprocessing
To ensure high-quality input data for the classifier, each signal undergoes noise reduction, normalization,
and feature extraction
C. CNN-Based Classification
A Convolutional Neural Network (CNN) is employed to classify plastic types (e.g., PE, PP, PS, PET)
based on their spectral signatures. The CNN automatically extracts spatial- spectral features without
manual feature engineering.

 IJSDR2510143 International Journal of Scientific Development and Research (IJSDR) www.ijsdr.org b374
ISSN:2455-2631                                         October 2025 IJSDR | Volume 10 Issue 10

D. Real-Time Detection

At runtime, the trained model is deployed on an embedded processor (Raspberry Pi). Incoming spectral

data are preprocessed and classified locally, and results are transmitted to the IoT cloud dashboard via

MQTT protocol.

E. IoT Cloud Transmission
Data transmission to the cloud is handled using the Message Queuing Telemetry Transport (MQTT)
protocol. The sensor node publishes JSON-formatted detection results to a broker, where visualization
dashboards (AWS IoT or Thing Speak) process and store the data.

Figure 3: Flowchart of Microplastic Detection System

V.EVALUATION PLAN

Evaluation metrics include:

          Accuracy: Percentage of correctly identified microplastic samples.
          Precision: Ratio of true positives to total predicted positives.
          Recall: Ratio of true positives to total actual positives.
          F1-Score: Harmonic mean of precision and recall.
          Latency: Time between signal capture and result output.
          Energy Efficiency: Power consumed per detection cycle.

VII. BUSINESS MODEL AND DEPLOYMENT ROADMAP

     The system will be sold at a low cost to schools, labs, and government groups.
     Extra income will come from cloud data services and maintenance.
     It will first be tested, then used in coastal areas, and later expanded to more places.
     The goal is to build a global network to track microplastic pollution.
     It helps protect the environment and supports clean water projects.

VIII. RESULTS AND DISCUSSION

The Smart Sensor System was tested both in the laboratory and in real marine environments to evaluate its
performance. The results showed that the system could detect and classify microplastics with an
accuracy of over 93%, identifying types such as
polyethylene (PE), polypropylene (PP), polystyrene (PS), and polyethylene terephthalate (PET). The CNN
model provided fast and reliable results, with an average response time of less than 1.5 seconds. Field
testing at locations like Chennai, Visakhapatnam, and Mumbai confirmed that the device performed well
under different water conditions, including variations in turbidity and temperature. The data were
transmitted securely to the IoT cloud dashboard, where users could view real-time results and pollution
levels. The system also consumed low power and operated continuously with high stability. Overall, the
study demonstrates that the Smart Sensor System is a practical, accurate, and efficient solution for real-
time microplastic detection and environmental monitoring.

IJSDR2510143 International Journal of Scientific Development and Research (IJSDR) www.ijsdr.org b376
ISSN:2455-2631                                    October 2025 IJSDR | Volume 10 Issue 10

Fig4: Presence of Microplastics in everyday life

IX. FUTURE SCOPE

Future developments will focus on integrating hyperspectral imaging for finer spectral resolution and the
use of solar-powered buoys for autonomous long-term deployment. Blockchain-based data provenance can
enhance transparency and reliability of environmental datasets. Moreover, incorporating federated learning
will allow distributed models to learn from multiple sensors without centralizing raw data, thus improving
privacy and scalability. Figure 5 provides a conceptual illustration of future deployment scenarios.

X. CONCLUSION

The Smart Sensor System helps to find and measure microplastics in water easily and quickly. It uses AI
and IoT to give accurate results in real time. The system worked well in different water conditions and
showed over 93% accuracy. Data were safely sent to the cloud for easy viewing and analysis.

This project shows that smart technology can help protect the environment and reduce plastic pollution in
a simple and low-cost way.
